## 1. Process notes

### 1.1 Source and currency
- **Official source.** Every Chapter 42 section was read on the official ncleg.gov chapter page (`EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/ByChapter/Chapter_42.html`), which prints each section's history line. No host copy (Justia, Casetext) was used for any row.
- **Currency.** Chapter 42's newest history-line entries are S.L. 2025-45, s. 10, 2025-52, s. 3(a) and 2025-54, s. 12.4(a) (all § 42-46). Every 2025 session law (S.L. 2025-1 to 2025-97) and every 2026 session law (S.L. 2026-1 to 2026-61; 62 onward return 404) was fetched and screened for 'G.S. 42-', 'Chapter 42', landlord, tenant, lessee, rental agreement and squatter terms. Hits that matter: S.L. 2025-45, -52, -54 (§ 42-46); S.L. 2025-88 (squatters, new G.S. 14-159.50 to 14-159.56, effective 2025-12-01); S.L. 2026-59, s. 52 (G.S. 62-110(g), pre-1989 mobile home parks; deprioritized). No 2026 act amends Chapter 42. Session laws are numbered continuously across regular and extra sessions, so the screen also covers any special session.
- **Compilation glitch (new instruction 46).** S.L. 2025-52 and 2025-54 (both approved 2025-07-02, both 'effective retroactively to September 9, 2024') each struck 'If the landlord is the prevailing party' from § 42-46(i)(3) and moved the frivolous-appeal fee rule into a new (i)(4) conditioned on the landlord prevailing. The official compilation prints '(4) court and the landlord is the prevailing party, delay.' followed by a '(5)' carrying S.L. 2024-47's older sentence. The rows follow the enacted text (`eviction-fees-nc` notes).
- **Effective dates (instruction 34).** S.L. 2024-47 became law 2024-09-09 over a veto (§§ 42-14.1, 42-46); S.L. 2025-45 approved 2025-07-01, Part X effective when it became law (§ 42-46(l)); S.L. 2025-88 effective 2025-12-01; S.L. 2023-5 became law 2023-03-19 (§ 42-14.6).
- **Stale kickoff lead.** § 42-45 was amended by S.L. 2019-161, s. 1(d) to strike 'member of the Armed Forces of the United States, the Active Guard and Reserve'; it now covers only military technicians under 10 U.S.C. § 10216. Servicemembers are protected by the N.C. Servicemembers Civil Relief Act (Chapter 127B, Article 4), which incorporates the federal SCRA.

### 1.2 How the text was obtained
- The shell cannot reach ncleg.gov (proxy 403). The built-in browser could: Taylor allowed ncleg.gov for the browser pane, and chapter pages and session laws were read verbatim through in-page scripts. Session-law amendment markers (`<s>` and `<u>`) were preserved as [-deleted-] and {+added+} when read.
- **Full-code search.** All 396 chapter pages listed on the General Statutes table of contents were loaded into the browser page (57.4 million characters, 0 errors) and searched with exact regular expressions, with each hit mapped back to its section number. The control term returned 0 and 'security deposit' returned 46 hits in 25 sections. This did the proof-of-absence and cross-title work directly, which is why **research mode was not needed** (§17).
- No section needed a paste from Taylor (§5a.2 never triggered).
- **Real lease (§15):** WebFetch of the NC REALTORS PDF on ncrealtors.org gave a paragraph-by-paragraph outline. Per instructions 6 and 45 it is a lead only; every point relied on rests on statute text read for this pass.

### 1.3 Section-open vs recall (instruction 22)
Every row was drafted with its section text in view in this session. The recall subset is empty. **No case law is relied on**; where North Carolina law turns on case law (waiver by accepting rent, penalty doctrine for early-termination fees, § 6-21.2's reach to leases, how § 42-12 and § 42-42 interact after a casualty, whether a lease may lengthen § 42-14 notice), the row says so (instruction 16).

## 2. Step 1 — tag first

### 2.1 Tagged NC as written (51)
`rent-payment`, `returned-payments`, `due-at-signing`, `application-of-payments`, `residential-use-only`, `existing-condition`, `permitted-occupants`, `no-disturbance`, `smoking-policy`, `utilities-responsibility`, `utility-service-continuity`, `utility-payment-evidence`, `acceptable-payment-methods`, `tenant-maintenance`, `no-sublet-assign`, `no-alterations`, `joint-liability`, `services-utilities-provided` (base), `utilities-paid-by-landlord`, `appliances-included`, `landlord-maintenance`, `landlords-access`, `possession-delay`, `surrender-end-of-term`, `early-termination`, `notices`, `governing-law`, `severability`, `entire-agreement`, `addendum-precedence`, `electronic-signatures`, `pet-insurance-requirement`, `assigned-parking-space`, `parking-vehicle-rules`, `keys`, `guest-policy`, `guest-policy-day-limit`, `common-area-use`, `fire-safety-grilling`, `landscaping-irrigation`, `snow-removal`, `inspection-rights`, `lead-based-paint`, `hoa-compliance`, `rental-application-accuracy`, and the variants `tenant-forward-proceedings-ca`, `holdover-ca`, `storage-space-ks-oh-ca`, `parking-ks-oh-ca`, `tenants-property-insurance-ks-oh-ca`, `default-by-tenant-ks-ne`.

Every tagged row has an `NC:` note naming the controlling section. The ones that matter:
- **`default-by-tenant-ks-ne` instead of `default-by-tenant`.** The shared clause's last sentence gives the prevailing party 'court costs and reasonable attorneys' fees and expenses'. North Carolina allows only the litigation costs listed in § 42-46(i) (filing fees, service costs, and attorneys' fees 'pursuant to a written lease' capped at 15%), makes it 'contrary to public policy' for a lease to provide for any other eviction litigation cost (§ 42-46(h)(3a)) and voids contrary terms (§ 42-46(h)(4)); the tenant side has no statutory basis (§ 6-21.6 excludes consumer contracts). The fee-sentence-free variant plus the opt-in `eviction-fees-nc` covers it. Decision 3 asks Taylor to confirm.
- **Instruction 33.** Without a forfeiture clause, forfeiture for nonpayment needs a demand and 10 days (§ 42-3); tender of rent and costs before judgment ends the case (§ 42-33). The no-cure route is Article 7 (criminal activity), which the shared carve-out preserves.
- **Variants over bases (parking, storage, renter's insurance).** The bases' blanket 'Landlord is not liable' would reach loss caused by a failure to repair; § 42-42(b) says the landlord is not released of its § 42-42 duties by any acceptance, and § 42-44(a) makes them enforceable by civil action. Same choice as GA decision 5.
- **`holdover-ca` instead of `holdover` (K.3).** No statutory multiplier (confirmed absent); damages for occupation (§ 42-28); month-to-month ends on 7 days' notice (§ 42-14).
- **`notices` and `electronic-signatures`.** Tagged; the UETA excludes default, cure and eviction notices (G.S. 66-313(e)(2)); `notices` designates no electronic method.
- **`returned-payments`.** $35 processing-fee cap for NSF checks (G.S. 25-3-506) gives 'maximum permitted by law' a referent for checks; no statute for failed ACH or card payments.
- **`utilities-paid-by-landlord`.** Its 'included in Monthly Rent' is the lease agreement G.S. 143-151.42(b)(2) needs for a master-metered post-1977 building.

All 335 other active lease clauses were listed and screened. None applies as written; each encodes its own state's statute. Closest analogues and what NC took instead: DV rows → `dv-lease-termination-nc` (NC's documents, safety plan and lock-change mechanics differ); casualty rows → `casualty-termination-nc` (modelled on GA/WY, displacing § 42-12); `authorized-person-contact-az`, `deceased-tenant-contact-tx` → `emergency-contact-nc` (NC's contact person is written into G.S. 28A-25-7); `crime-free-addendum-az`, `serious-misconduct-prohibition-ga` → `criminal-activity-nc` (built on the § 42-59 definition); `utility-billing-disclosure-az` → `utility-billing-nc`; smoke-detector rows → `smoke-co-alarms-nc`; `periodic-tenancy-notice-wy` → `periodic-tenancy-notice-nc`; `tenant-repair-agreement-tx/-nv` not copied (§ 42-42(b) allows only a *subsequent* separate written contract with other consideration, which a lease clause cannot be); `electronic-notice-consent-tx`, `electronic-notice-addendum-fl` not copied (G.S. 66-313(e)(2)).

## 4. Layout and placement requirements (instruction 28)

**Code-wide typography search** (boldface, bold type, bold print, capital letters, conspicuous, underlin*, point type, separate document, separately signed, within 300 characters of lease, tenant, lessee or rental agreement): 13 hits in 9 sections. The only Chapter 42 hits are 'posted conspicuously' (§ 42-25.9(e), abandonment notice) and 'conspicuous part of the premises' (§ 42-29, service). The rest are vacation rentals (§ 42A-11), lease-option contracts (G.S. 47G-2, 14-point bold), leases of goods, rental referral agencies and unrelated titles. **No bold, capitals, type-size or separate-document rule for residential leases.**

| Rule | Requirement | Where it lives |
|---|---|---|
| § 42-46(e)-(g), (i)(3) | Eviction administrative fees and attorneys' fees only 'pursuant to a written lease' | `eviction-fees-nc` |
| § 42-26(c) | Partial-payment non-waiver only if 'the lease' provides it | `partial-payment-nonwaiver-nc` |
| § 42-42.1(a) | Utility charge-back only 'pursuant to a written rental agreement' | `utility-billing-nc` |
| § 42-50 | Deposit location or bond insurer disclosed **within 30 days after the lease term begins** | `security-deposit-holding-nc` |
| § 42-42(a)(5), (7) | Alarms operable and fresh batteries **at the beginning of each tenancy** (battery allocation changeable only by written agreement) | `smoke-co-alarms-nc` |
| G.S. 66-313(e)(2) | Default, cure and eviction notices for a primary residence are outside the UETA: deliver on paper | `edu-electronic-notices-nc` |

**Omission sanctions that forfeit money** (second half of instruction 28):
- willful failure of the deposit, bond or 30-day notice rules voids the right to retain any of the deposit (§ 42-55);
- no written lease clause, no eviction administrative fees or attorneys' fees (§ 42-46(e)-(g), (i)(3));
- a late-fee or other fee term contrary to § 42-46 is void (§ 42-46(h)(4));
- no lease provision on casualty leaves the tenant's § 42-12 surrender right in place.

These add to the **Addendum M.12** case for a `formatting` field; North Carolina adds no new value to it.

| Row | Right | Wrong or missing | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| `late-fee-limit-nc` | The $15-or-5% cap; the five-day threshold | No weekly-rent cap; not 'calendar' days counted from the day after the due date (S.L. 2024-47); nothing on once-per-payment, no deduction from a later payment, no late fee for landlord-billed water or sewer, subsidized tenant's share; was not linked to the shared `late-fee` | Rewritten as the NC late-fee clause; activated; supersedes `late-fee`; `topic_key` normalised from `late-fee-limit-nc` to `late-fee`; prior text kept in notes |

**Pattern holds again:** one real fact, part of the operative rule missed.

## 6. Decisions for Taylor

| # | Question | Recommendation | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Prepaid last-month rent: deposit or rent?** North Carolina's Act caps 'security deposits' (§ 42-51(b)) but never defines the term and never mentions prepaid rent. | Treat it the way you decided for Georgia: money paid in advance toward the last month counts toward the cap and is held and returned with the deposit, unless the lease expressly makes it Rent for a named calendar month. Add that sentence to `security-deposit-cap-nc`. | Consistency with GA; it is the safer reading of a silent statute, because if a court treats unapplied prepaid rent as security, a landlord who collected two months' deposit plus last month's rent would be over the cap. Today the row says nothing either way. |
| 2 | **Offer an opt-in stipulated holdover rate for North Carolina (like `holdover-rate-ga`)?** | No, unless you want parity with Georgia. | North Carolina already gives 'damages for the occupation of the premises since the cessation of the estate' in the summary ejectment itself (§ 42-28), and a stipulated rate would rest only on penalty case law. |
| 3 | **Confirm `default-by-tenant-ks-ne` (no fee sentence) plus opt-in `eviction-fees-nc`, instead of the shared `default-by-tenant`.** | Confirm. | The shared fee sentence provides for uncapped fees and 'expenses' that § 42-46(h)(3a) makes contrary to public policy for eviction complaints, and nothing supports the tenant side. The NC clause states exactly what the statute allows. |
| 4 | **Keep the four new opt-in clauses** (`criminal-activity-nc`, `casualty-termination-nc`, `periodic-tenancy-notice-nc`, `emergency-contact-nc`)? | Keep. | Each gives the landlord a right it otherwise lacks or forfeits: a no-cure lease breach for criminal activity (§ 42-59(2)); a casualty rule in place of § 42-12's narrow surrender right; a longer notice than § 42-14's 7 days; a named contact for G.S. 28A-25-7. The NC REALTORS lease has the first two. |

## 8. Integrity and screens
- **CSV:** 1,185 rows; every row has 16 fields (re-read with the `csv` module); no duplicate ids; no dangling `supersedes`; no display collisions (programmatic check over every active `supersedes` pair, all states); no blank status; no active row with blank `states` except the intentional `security-deposit-return` parent. Line endings CRLF, as in the input. 52 existing rows changed: 51 tags (states, an appended ' | NC: ...' note, `last_checked` 2026-09-28) plus the dormant rewrite. For every tagged row, `bodyText`, `rule_type`, `content_type`, `supersedes` and the pre-existing notes are unchanged (checked programmatically); every other existing row is unchanged.
- **Counts:** NC 0 → 111 (68 lease clauses, 43 education; all VERIFIED). Every other state's active count unchanged: AZ 109, CA 157, CO 116, FL 107, GA 103, KS 129, MN 139, ND 122, NE 123, NJ 85, NV 121, OH 97, SD 99, TX 135, WY 106.

The rest were deprioritized layers, now cited as such in `edu-vacation-rental-scope-nc`'s notes: § 42-1 (commercial profit-share leases), § 42-7 and Article 2 (§§ 42-15 to 42-25: agricultural, turpentine, mining and timber leases), § 42-14.3 (manufactured home communities). **None owed.** No shared row's `bodyText`, `rule_type` or `content_type` changed. Every change to an existing shared row is an added `NC` tag with an `NC:` note, which is a states-only change under §5a.1.

### 9.1 Flags for Claude CLI
**None.** No specific defect was found in another state's row. (The `pet-policy` pet-removal sentence is wrong for North Carolina because of §§ 42-25.7 to 42-25.9; whether it is right for its six tagged states depends on each state's own self-help law, which this pass did not read, so it is not flagged.)

### Vouches given
- **2026-10-03:** `surrender-end-of-term` (CA's proposal), vouched for NC; reasoning in "Circle-back checks (SOP 1.45)" at the end of this log.

## 10. Findings worth Taylor's attention
1. **North Carolina polices landlord fees more tightly than any state so far.** Late fees, eviction administrative fees and litigation costs are a closed list with caps, most need a written lease, and anything else is void (§ 42-46). That is why the shared `default-by-tenant` and `late-fee` were not tagged.
2. **The deposit's bank notice is a forfeiture trap.** Telling the tenant where the deposit is held within 30 days of the term's start is statutory, and a willful miss voids the right to keep any of the deposit (§§ 42-50, 42-55).
3. **Month-to-month tenancies end on seven days' notice** (§ 42-14), unless the lease says otherwise (`periodic-tenancy-notice-nc`).
4. **Eviction and default notices should not go by e-mail or text:** the UETA excludes them (G.S. 66-313(e)(2)). The NC REALTORS lease allows electronic notices generally.
5. **Service animals get a flat right with no direct-threat limit and no species limit** (G.S. 168-4.2), plus criminal penalties both ways (§ 168-4.5).
6. **Self-help is barred, including over pets and belongings** (§§ 42-25.6 to 42-25.9); the shared pet clause's removal sentence would be void.
7. **New in 2025:** the squatter procedure (G.S. 14-159.50 ff., from 2025-12-01) and the renter's-insurance limits (§ 42-46(l)). **New in 2024:** local governments may not mandate voucher acceptance (§ 42-14.1(b)).
8. **The official compilation misprints § 42-46(i)(4)-(5)** (instruction 46).
9. **Servicemembers are now protected under Chapter 127B**, not § 42-45 (which covers only military technicians since 2019).

## 15. Real-lease comparison (gap-discovery source 2)

The lease is a lead only (instruction 6); every point below rests on primary text read for this pass.

Charity rule: no statute located (rests on Real Estate Commission rules, not read) |

## Decisions that need Taylor (short list)

1. **Prepaid last-month rent** — recommend the Georgia rule (counts toward the cap unless the lease makes it Rent for a named month); add one sentence to `security-deposit-cap-nc`.
2. **Opt-in holdover rate for NC** — recommend no (§ 42-28 already gives occupation damages).
3. **`default-by-tenant-ks-ne` + `eviction-fees-nc` instead of the shared `default-by-tenant`** — recommend confirm (§ 42-46(h)(3a)).
4. **Keep the four new opt-ins** (`criminal-activity-nc`, `casualty-termination-nc`, `periodic-tenancy-notice-nc`, `emergency-contact-nc`) — recommend keep.

**Integrity:** 1,185 rows (1,126 + 59 new; 1 dormant row rewritten and activated); NC 111 active (all VERIFIED); every other state's count unchanged (AZ 109, CA 157, CO 116, FL 107, GA 103, KS 129, MN 139, ND 122, NE 123, NJ 85, NV 121, OH 97, SD 99, TX 135, WY 106); no duplicate ids; no dangling `supersedes`; no display collisions; 16 fields on every row.

## 18. Sync note (Claude Code, 2026-09-28)

- Installed as delivered, with one correction: `security-deposit-holding-nc`'s NC note read "contrast GA N.C. Gen. Stat. § 44-7-36". That is a Georgia section (O.C.G.A. § 44-7-36, Georgia's small-landlord exemption) that the programmatic citation-format pass relabeled. The note now reads "contrast GA O.C.G.A. § 44-7-36". Notes only; no clause text changed. A scan of every NC row found no other section from another state carrying the NC prefix.
- Spot-checked against official ncleg.gov text: §§ 42-14, 42-46(a)-(b), 42-50, 42-51(b), 42-52. Each matched the rows that rely on it.

- **Taylor's answers to §6 (2026-09-28):** decision 1, yes, same as GA: `security-deposit-cap-nc` now counts prepaid last-month rent toward the cap unless the lease makes it Rent for a named calendar month (notes updated in `due-at-signing` and `edu-security-deposit-rules-nc` too). Decision 2, yes, same as GA: new opt-in `holdover-rate-nc`, text identical to `holdover-rate-ga`, written at sync after reading N.C. Gen. Stat. §§ 42-4, 42-26, 42-28 and all of § 42-46 on ncleg.gov (the penalty test is case law, not read; builder note to keep the rate near the daily rental value). Decisions 3 and 4 are still open.
- **Decision 3 (2026-09-28): confirmed.** NC uses `default-by-tenant-ks-ne` plus `eviction-fees-nc`, not the shared `default-by-tenant`. Taylor also wants `eviction-fees-nc` on every NC lease by default; recorded in the row's notes (the app's 'include by default' setting is per landlord).
- **Decision 4 (2026-09-28): keep all four** opt-in clauses (`criminal-activity-nc`, `casualty-termination-nc`, `periodic-tenancy-notice-nc`, `emergency-contact-nc`) as built. All four §6 decisions are now closed.

## Propagated shared-row edit, 2026-10-02 (Taylor, at the Michigan sync)

Not a re-audit; nothing else in this state was reviewed.

**Propagation note (uniform edit, rule 62): `snow-removal` rewritten.** Old: 'Unless Landlord provides snow removal service, Tenant is responsible for prompt, reasonable removal of snow and ice from any walkway, driveway, porch, or entrance at the property that Tenant uses, to help keep those areas safe and passable.' New: 'Unless Landlord provides snow removal, Tenant will promptly remove snow and ice from the areas of the property Tenant uses for walking, parking and access. This does not include areas shared with other residents.' Why: Taylor found the list of areas too specific (properties differ, and a list invites arguments about what it covers), and Michigan's sync showed the clause should say outright that shared areas stay with the landlord. The edit only narrows the tenant's duty; this state's existing note on the row still holds.

## Retro checks (SOP 1.21), 2026-10-02

**Date:** 2026-10-02. **Scope:** a scalpel, not a re-audit (rule 1). Only the 18 [Retro] rules and the 7 targeted fixes in the PROMPT were checked; nothing else was reopened. **Settings:** Opus, high effort. Research mode was not used, because the full official code and constitution were loaded and searched directly (rule 9).

**Inputs:** the attached files were the only source of truth.
- `lease-clauses.csv` has 2,444 rows, 17 columns, CRLF, matching the PROMPT.
- The attached files matched my working copies by SHA-256 when they were re-sent mid-run.
- Old outputs were deleted first: `lease-clause-decision-log-NC.md`, `lease-clause-decision-log-named-topic-checklist.md` and `lease-clauses-NC-sync.csv`.
- Where this chat's 2026-09-28 work differs from the attached library (for example the 2026-09-29 scrub and the propagated edits), the attached files were followed.

**Sources:**
- *Sections:* 57 sections were read on ncleg.gov in the built-in browser. Each was fetched twice, with matching hashes and the heading number checked. 48 are saved to the NC retro source folder, each SHA-256-matched to the browser text. The other 9 have a browser hash only and nothing rests on them for a change: §§ 42-4, 42-9, 42-38, 42-40, 42-42.3, 42-64, 6-21.3, 6-21.6, 7A-228.
- *Full-text corpus:* all 396 chapters on the General Statutes table of contents (0 fetch errors), plus the NC Constitution decoded as windows-1252. 57,561,854 characters. Control term 0 hits; `security deposit` 46/25, the same as on 2026-09-28. Every battery, its known positive (real or synthetic) and a heading-only screen are in `battery_NC_retro_2026-10-02.md`.
- *Court rules:* the General Rules of Practice (codified 6 August 2025) were parsed in the browser with pdf.js. Only the full-text hash was recorded, a weaker method (rule 14), and the text is not on disk.
- *Session laws:* no new NC session law since 2026-09-28; 2026 still ends at S.L. 2026-61.

**Delta:** `lease-clauses-NC-retro-delta.csv` has 47 rows: 33 changed and 14 new. NC goes from 111 to 123 active rows (67 lease clauses, 56 education rows). No other state's count changed, and no shared row's `bodyText`, `rule_type` or `content_type` changed.

### Proposed SOP changes
1. **Rule 47:** add "check the state's debt-collection statute". Where it reaches 'any person' collecting a consumer debt (NC § 75-50(3)), collecting under a void fee term carries per-violation penalties even though the term itself is merely void. This turns a void-only state into a penalty state.
2. **Rule 19:** wrap every alternation in a group before adding a proximity limb. NC's B27form and B48 bound the proximity to the first and last alternatives only. The result was a broader screen, not a false empty, but the reverse error would hide a hit.
3. **Rule 39:** where court rules are published only as a long PDF the session cannot save to disk, allow recording the full-text hash with the rule sections relied on quoted in the log, and name it a weaker method. This makes rule 14's existing weaker-method clause explicit for court rules.
4. **Rule 53 (holdover triggers), for GA's and other states' attention:** `holdover-rate-ga`'s "earlier termination under this Lease or applicable law" also reaches a termination for nonpayment of rent. In a state that caps late fees, as North Carolina does, a landlord-set daily charge after a nonpayment termination can work as a second, repeating late charge. Each state that adopted the extended trigger should check it against its late-fee rule. The NC version excludes nonpayment terminations.

| # | Rule / fix | Verdict | What was read | Rows changed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| — | Retro rules | None listed in this PROMPT | — | — |
| 1 | **Fix 1**, rule 62 vetting of CA's proposed `surrender-end-of-term` qualifier ("unless applicable law entitles Tenant to remain") | **Vouched, no change needed.** The qualified wording is lawful and accurate in NC. The current wording states a duty NC law does not back in a few narrow cases. | §§ 42-26(a), 42-28, 42-14, 42-25.6, 42-34(b), 42-37.1(b)-(c), 42-37.2(a), 42-37.3, 42-42.2, 47A-36(a), 42-14.3(a), 127B-28(a); NC's own note segment on the row | None (shared text not edited; NC stays tagged) |

**Fix 1, the reasoning.**

*Does the current wording state a duty NC law doesn't back?* Mostly no, with a few narrow exceptions. NC has no just-cause rule (`edu-no-for-cause-eviction-nc`). A tenant who holds over after the term "has expired", or after the estate has ceased under the lease, is removable by summary ejectment after a demand for surrender (§ 42-26(a)(1)-(2)). The landlord recovers damages for occupation "since the cessation of the estate" (§ 42-28). So the general duty to surrender at the end of the Lease is backed. But NC law lets a tenant stay past the Lease's end in these cases, and the unqualified "immediately" overstates the duty there:
- **Condominium conversion:** "No tenant or subtenant may be required to vacate upon less than 90 days' notice", except for default grounds, and failure to give the notice "is a defense to an action for possession" (§ 47A-36(a)).
- **Retaliation:** if the court finds the ejectment retaliatory it "shall deny the request for ejectment" (§ 42-37.2(a)), and the tenant cannot waive this (§ 42-37.3). This can reach a periodic tenancy ended by notice. It does not reach a fixed term with no option to renew, because the landlord prevails on that holdover (§ 42-37.1(c)(2)).
- **Victim status:** a landlord "shall not terminate a tenancy, fail to renew a tenancy" based substantially on a tenant's status as a victim of domestic violence, sexual assault or stalking (§ 42-42.2). The statute names no remedy, so whether a tenant can rely on it to stay is a case-law question, not read.
- **Appeal stay:** an appeal stays execution of a summary ejectment judgment while the tenant pays rent into the clerk (§ 42-34(b)).
- **Federal SCRA:** its protections, which § 127B-28(a) incorporates, may stay proceedings. The federal text was not read.
- **Manufactured-home community conversion:** 180 days' notice "regardless of the term of the tenancy" (§ 42-14.3(a)). This is a deprioritized layer.

None of these makes the current clause void or dangerous in NC. It is a duty sentence, not a self-help right, and a residential tenant can be removed only by court process anyway (§ 42-25.6). The exposure is wording, as CA found.

*Rule 62, question 1: can the qualified text be read to drop a cure or notice NC requires?* No. It narrows only the tenant's promise. It removes no demand, notice or court step: the § 42-26(a) demand for surrender and the § 42-36.2 writ procedure are unaffected.

*Rule 62, question 2: does it change what the clause promises in NC?* Only by matching it to the law in the cases above. NC's note segment on this row has no "gives up" or "kept on purpose" language, and no NC note relies on an unqualified surrender duty. `holdover-ca` (month-to-month continuation "terminable only as provided by law") and `holdover-rate-nc` (charge "after this Lease ends") read consistently with it. The qualifier also does not let a tenant stay merely because a lease clause allows it, because it is limited to "applicable law". NC needs no override and supports the edit as uniform.

### Vouches given
*Claude Code: per rule 62, please file this line under NC log §9 (Propagation notes), subheading "Vouches given".*
- `surrender-end-of-term`, CA proposal 2026-10-03 (add "unless applicable law entitles Tenant to remain" to the surrender sentence): **vouched, no change needed for NC.** It is lawful and accurate here. It drops no NC notice or cure, and it reopens no route NC gave up on purpose. NC law lets a tenant remain past the Lease's end in narrow cases: § 47A-36(a) conversion notice, §§ 42-37.2(a) and 42-37.3 retaliation, the § 42-34(b) appeal stay, and possibly § 42-42.2. Read section-open 2026-10-03.

### Proposed SOP changes
None.

"Tenant will surrender possession of the property and return all keys to Landlord immediately" now ends ", unless applicable law entitles Tenant to remain." ## Cross-state decisions, 2026-10-04 (Taylor; central check by Claude Code)

- **New federal row:** `edu-cares-act-notice` is now tagged for NC. Under 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c), a landlord of a property with a federally backed mortgage, or in a covered federal housing program, may not require the tenant to vacate until 30 days after a notice to vacate; whether that still applies after the 2020 moratorium is unsettled. Any NC row that already mentions the CARES Act stays; the federal row is the library's standard explanation.
- **Shared-text edits merged centrally** (Taylor approved merging now; each only narrows the tenant's obligations or defers to applicable law, so it can't breach NC's law; to be confirmed at NC's end-of-run consistency pass):
- `smoking-policy`: Tenant pays for smoking damage caused by "Tenant, an occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant", no longer by anyone's smoking.
- `no-alterations`: the last sentence adds that the clause "does not change who owns an installation that applicable law makes Tenant's property".
- `addendum-precedence`: a disclosure, notice or addendum "that applicable law says controls over this Lease" now controls, as one the law requires already did.

Key statutes read (quotes):
- § 42-25.8: 'Any lease or contract provision contrary to this Article shall be void as against public policy.'
- § 42-37.3: 'Any waiver by a tenant or a member of his household of the rights and remedies created by this Article is void as contrary to public policy.'
- § 42-42(b): 'The landlord is not released of his obligations under any part of this section by the tenant's explicit or implicit acceptance of the landlord's failure to provide premises complying with this section'
- § 42-44(c): 'The tenant may not unilaterally withhold rent prior to a judicial determination of a right to do so.'
- § 42-45.1(d): 'The provisions of this section may not be waived or modified by agreement of the parties.'
- § 42-46(h)(4): 'Any provision of a residential rental agreement contrary to the provisions of this section is against the public policy of this State and therefore void and unenforceable.'
- § 22B-10: jury waivers 'unconscionable as a matter of law'; 'This section does not prohibit parties from entering into agreements to arbitrate'
- § 1-569.6(a): an agreement 'to submit to arbitration any existing or subsequent controversy ... is valid, enforceable, and irrevocable'
- § 42-52: the landlord may not 'retain an amount from the security deposit which exceeds his actual damages'
- § 42-42.3(c): 'The protected tenant shall bear the expense of changing the locks.'

# Topic gaps pass (end-of-run consistency pass, 2026-11-07): Desktop's log

## North Carolina topic gaps log (end-of-run consistency pass)

SOP 1.98. Desktop pass of 2026-11-07. Cumulative to this delivery: 250 of 250 topics answered (250 new rows, 29 tagged rows). Generated 2026-11-07 17:25.

### 1. Sources, channel and currency
#### 1.1 Channel
- Step A (2026-11-07): `lease-clauses.csv` decompressed from the attachment, 55,756,124 bytes, SHA-256 887af16e36e49d8306e984a7eb877d574d9ec38bce53c3af7f8c133020f9743b (match); 14,729 rows, 14,617 active, 124 active rows carrying NC (match); `missing-topics-NC.md` lists 250 topics (match). Old outputs deleted before starting.
- Primary text: the Claude Code corpus `corpus-NC-20261106.tar.xz`, SHA-256 f8b91ac615a0487655164c0c0839fe6bd88d1abaa6df73bcd80115fe2da36ea9 (matches the attached `.sha256`). Its own check (`verify_nc.py`, run in an isolated interpreter, rule 24 [GU 3]) reported 270 files in the manifest, 33 source pages checked, all OK. Held: the whole text of all 397 General Statutes chapters (STATUTES.TXT, 41,706 headings, each with its history line), every session law of 2024 (S.L. 2024-1 to 2024-58), 2025 (2025-1 to 2025-97) and 2026 (2026-1 to 2026-62), with struck text as [-...-] and new text as {+...+}; the Constitution; 04 NCAC 11, 10A NCAC 41, 11 NCAC 05, 15A NCAC 18A, 21 NCAC 58 and 26 NCAC 04.
- Live cross-check (rule 24): the official site was reachable in the built-in browser on Taylor's desktop. Six sections were fetched from www.ncleg.gov BySection pages (HTTP 200) and compared with the corpus by whitespace-free SHA-256 (page text from "§<section>." to the end; corpus heading, body and history; weaker than a byte copy because the corpus heading omits the period after the number and prints the history on its own line): § 42-51 (1,394 characters), § 42-46 (5,919), § 42-42 (6,491), § 160D-1207 (6,474), § 22B-10 (369) and § 42-45.1 (2,024), all six matching. The corpus is therefore used as the reading copy.
- Pass tools (workspace, not from the corpus): a section splitter that locates each heading by its text in catchline order (catchlines.tsv line numbers drift and are not used), runs each section to the next heading, splits § 1A-1 and § 8C-1 into their rules, the Constitution by article and section (155 units) and the NCAC files by rule heading (counts 15A NCAC 18A 932, 04 NCAC 11 455, 21 NCAC 58 388, 10A NCAC 41 222, 11 NCAC 05 79, 26 NCAC 04 14, each matching a loose heading count); proof: no non-structural line of STATUTES.TXT is left outside a section. A logged battery engine (control in every run, refuses a run without a positive, prints every hit, heading-only hits and inflection warnings, records the corpus hash) and a row checker (fields, groups, basis, citations held, every single-quoted statute fragment matched to the cited section, batteries logged).
- Not held, flagged where a row needs them: court rules and AOC forms (nccourts.gov refused the staging script with 403); local ordinances (rule 3); case law (leads only); NCAC chapters outside the six above; federal law (see 1.3).
- Fetched outside the corpus this pass: the six live section pages and the live table of contents above (read in the browser, not saved); and, for `edu-fire-code-standard-nc`, the Office of the State Fire Marshal's letter of March 31, 2025 on the 2024 State Building Code effective date and its March 2026 Engineering Newsletter (ncosfm.gov, web fetch 2026-11-07, not saved; the certification under S.L. 2025-2, s. 5.12(b) is not in the corpus). For the next corpus: the OSFM certification letter, when issued; and 02 NCAC 34 (Structural Pest Control Committee) and 02 NCAC 09L (Pesticide Board), needed by `edu-pest-control-notice-nc` (tried 2026-11-07: the corpus does not hold them; the OAH chapter download at oah.nc.gov served a five-page periodic-review list, not the rule text; a web search found only unofficial mirrors, not used).

#### 1.2 Currency
- Compilation statement, corpus and live (www.ncleg.gov/Laws/GeneralStatutesTOC, 2026-11-07): "The General Statutes include changes through S.L. 2026-30".
- Every relied-on section's history line was read, and every held 2024-2026 act citing it listed (`nc.py cur`). Every G.S. section that S.L. 2026-31 to 2026-62 amends, rewrites, repeals or adds to was listed from the acts' text (990 act-section pairs; 325 already shown in the section's history line, the rest uncompiled); a row relying on an uncompiled change applies the act's text and gives its effective date.
- Effective-date sections read for the acts that cite Chapter 42 or 42A:
  - S.L. 2024-47 (§§ 42-14.1, 42-29, 42-46): became law over the Governor's veto on September 9, 2024; s. 10 sets July 1, 2024 for ss. 3-5 and October 1, 2024 for s. 9, and calls the s. 8 amendments (§ 42-46) clarifying.
  - S.L. 2025-45, s. 10 (§ 42-46(l), required renters insurance): effective when it became law (s. 14), approved July 1, 2025.
  - S.L. 2025-52, s. 3(a) and S.L. 2025-54, s. 12.4(a) (§ 42-46(i)): both retroactive to September 9, 2024 (s. 3.(b); s. 12.4.(b)); approved July 2, 2025. The compiled § 42-46(i)(4) prints the known garble "(4) court and the landlord is the prevailing party, delay."; rows relying on § 42-46(i) follow the enacted text of both acts.
  - S.L. 2024-26 (§ 42A-4), S.L. 2024-54 (§§ 42-31, 42-34; Part 2 October 1, 2025, Part 3 October 1, 2024), S.L. 2025-25 (§ 42-15; section-by-section dates), S.L. 2025-88 (cites §§ 42-26, 42-59 and 42A-4 in the new unauthorized-occupant removal article; effective December 1, 2025; amends no Chapter 42 section), S.L. 2026-34 (§ 42A-39; effective when it became law, offenses December 1, 2026) and S.L. 2026-59 (cites § 42-59(3) in its rewrite of § 62-110(g), uncompiled; ratified August 6, 2026, approved August 11, 2026, s. 53.(b) "Except as otherwise provided, this act is effective when it becomes law").

#### 1.3 Federal law
Taylor was asked (rule 12) whether the pass may save the standard federal sections the canvass needs (uscode.house.gov and eCFR through the built-in browser), with the recommendation yes. Until then rows cite no U.S.C. or C.F.R. section the pass has not read: they point to the library rows that state the federal rule, marked "FEDERAL:" (gaps log §4 class decision 8).

### 3. Batteries

### 4. Decisions and questions for Taylor
#### Class decisions

Cite a decision in a row's notes as "gaps log §4 class decision N". Every quote below was printed whole from the corpus with `nc.py sec`; re-print before relying on it in a row.

**1. Review status (SOP Part 5, Taylor 2026-10-15).** A doubt only case law could settle: the row states the doubt and the cautious course and stays VERIFIED. NEEDS_REVIEW only where the answer rests on a primary source this pass can't reach (a court rule, an AOC form, a local ordinance, an NCAC chapter not in the corpus). Name the source in the body ("the AOC form was not read for this library").

**2. Statutory non-waiver wordings (quote the one that applies; never paraphrase into "void"):**
- Article 2A (Ejectment of residential tenants, §§ 42-25.6 to 42-25.9): 'Any lease or contract provision contrary to this Article shall be void as against public policy.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-25.8)
- Article 4A (Retaliatory eviction): 'Any waiver by a tenant or a member of his household of the rights and remedies created by this Article is void as contrary to public policy.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-37.3)
- Article 5 (Residential Rental Agreements Act): no general anti-waiver section. § 42-42(b): 'The landlord is not released of his obligations under any part of this section by the tenant's explicit or implicit acceptance of the landlord's failure to provide premises complying with this section' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42(b)), with the 'subsequent written contract' exception in the same subsection. § 42-41 makes rent and § 42-42(a) 'mutually dependent'. § 42-44(c): 'The tenant may not unilaterally withhold rent prior to a judicial determination of a right to do so.' § 42-44(d): 'A violation of this Article shall not constitute negligence per se.'
- § 42-45(c) (military technicians only: the section reaches 'Any military technician under section 10216 of Title 10 of the United States Code'; servicemembers generally come under N.C. Gen. Stat. § 127B-28 and FEDERAL: the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act): 'The provisions of this section may not be waived or modified by the agreement of the parties under any circumstances.'
- § 42-45.1(d) (DV/sexual assault/stalking): 'The provisions of this section may not be waived or modified by agreement of the parties.'
- § 42-46(h)(4): 'Any provision of a residential rental agreement contrary to the provisions of this section is against the public policy of this State and therefore void and unenforceable.'
- Tenant Security Deposit Act (§§ 42-50 to 42-56): no anti-waiver section; the limits are stated as mandatory ('shall be permitted only', 'shall not exceed'), and § 42-55 voids the right to retain on willful noncompliance.
- § 22B-10 (jury waivers): 'Any provision in a contract requiring a party to the contract to waive his right to a jury trial is unconscionable as a matter of law and the provision shall be unenforceable.' It continues: 'This section does not prohibit parties from entering into agreements to arbitrate or engage in other forms of alternative dispute resolution.'
- § 22B-3: an agreement requiring litigation or arbitration 'in another state' is void and unenforceable (quote the section's own words when used).
Where no statute bars a waiver, say so, give the default rule first, then: "Whether a lease term waiving it is enforceable is case law, which this library has not reviewed; the library offers no waiver clause" (cautious course).

**3. Default rule first.** For every waiver or "may the lease change this" topic: state the statutory rule, then whether the statute lets the parties vary it (its own words: "unless otherwise agreed", "written agreement to the contrary"), then the cautious course.

**4. Scope.** Article 5 (§§ 42-38 to 42-49): 'This Article determines the rights, obligations, and remedies under a rental agreement for a dwelling unit within this State.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-38). It 'shall not apply to vacation rentals entered into under Chapter 42A' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-39(a1)) or to 'any dwelling furnished without charge or rent' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-39(b)); the old hotel exclusion, § 42-39(a), was repealed by S.L. 2023-5, s. 1.5 (effective March 19, 2023). The Tenant Security Deposit Act: 'The provisions of this Article shall apply to all persons, firms, or corporations engaged in the business of renting or managing residential dwelling units, excluding single rooms, on a weekly, monthly or annual basis.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-56). Vacation rentals: Chapter 42A (§ 42A-18 for its deposits). State a scope limit only where the topic turns on it.

**5. Local rules (item 4).** Counties and cities may not regulate rent amounts and may not require landlords to accept tenants paying with federal housing assistance (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-14.1(a), (b), as rewritten by S.L. 2024-47, s. 7); exceptions in § 42-14.1(c). Rental permits, registration and periodic inspections are limited by N.C. Gen. Stat. § 160D-1207 (read it whole before stating its terms). Local minimum housing codes otherwise apply (N.C. Gen. Stat. §§ 160D-1201 to 160D-1208). Each local-limit row says what remains local, flags local ordinances as not researched for this library, and points to `edu-local-preemption-nc`.

**6. Last month's rent (item 3; NC decision 1, Taylor 2026-09-28).** The Act does not define "security deposit". A "last month's Monthly Rent" collected at signing counts toward the § 42-51(b) cap unless the lease makes it Rent for a named calendar month. `deposit-last-month-rent` follows this, citing N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-51(b) and `edu-security-deposit-rules-nc`; any reason the statute reads otherwise goes to §4 for Taylor, not into the row.

**7. Not held / not researched.** Court rules and AOC forms (nccourts.gov refused the script with 403; flag, don't resolve). Local ordinances (flag). Cases (leads only, `___ N.C. ___ (20__)`, never a basis). NCAC chapters other than 04 NCAC 11, 10A NCAC 41, 11 NCAC 05, 15A NCAC 18A, 21 NCAC 58 and 26 NCAC 04 (flag, NEEDS_REVIEW only if the answer rests on one). Statutes outside the corpus's 397 chapters do not exist for this pass; if a cited chapter is missing, the row's verdict is "Not located" naming it (rule 36).

**8. Federal law.** Mark "FEDERAL:". Until Taylor approves saving federal sections, cite no U.S.C. or C.F.R. section the pass has not read: point to the library row that states the federal rule (for example `edu-cares-act-notice`, the fair-housing and lead rows), "FEDERAL: see `<id>`". A row that needs a federal rule no library row states says so and stays VERIFIED on its NC answer, with the federal point in §7 of the log.

**9. Citations and quotes.** `N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-51(b)`; range `N.C. Gen. Stat. §§ 42-50 to 42-56`; `N.C. Gen. Stat. § 1A-1, Rule 68.1`; `S.L. 2025-52, s. 3(a)` with the act's effective date; `N.C. Const. art. I, § 19`; `21 NCAC 58A .0107`, `04 NCAC 11 R18-06`. Single quotes only for statute words, each followed by its citation in parentheses; double quotes for library text, search terms, headings and history lines. No bare "§ 42-51" in notes. Bodies are plain English with no single-quoted statute text.

**10. Tag first (rule 26).** Before writing a row, run `nc.py topic KEY`. If an existing shared education or clause row answers the topic and reads correctly under NC law in every sentence, tag it (object kind "tag": the NC segment says why it applies, with quotes and cites). A tag changes only `states` and `notes`. Never tag a row that needs an edit to be right for NC; write an NC row instead and say why in the notes.

**11. Three buckets (rule 55).** A clause row needs `lease_clause_basis` = `REQUIRED_DISCLOSURE: <cite>`, `CONSTRAINED_TERM` or `SERVES_LANDLORD`, and a clause group from the 12. Education rows: blank basis, group from the CSV's existing names (Building & Safety is education-only; Compliance & Prohibited Terms is used for waiver and void-term rows). Default to an education row. Write a new clause only where NC law requires lease text, or where other states' rows on the topic offer an optional clause that NC law allows and nothing in NC's existing rows serves (rule 54); a lawful option the library declines gets an education row saying it is lawful and not offered, and why.

**12. Topic ownership.** Each topic is answered once, in its own slice, keyed by its own `topic_key` (rule 57: one subject per row). Point to another slice's topic by its predictable id `edu-<topic_key>-nc`; the assembler checks every pointer resolves.

**13. Ids and fields.** Education `edu-<topic_key>-nc`; clause `<topic_key>-nc`; if taken, add a word, never a number. `states` NC; `is_active` TRUE; `supersedes` blank; `effective_from` and `last_checked` 2026-11-07; `choice_group` and `is_default` blank unless a choice group is joined; `rule_type` RECOMMENDED, REQUIRED, CONSTRAINED, PROHIBITED or CONDITIONAL as other states' rows on the topic use it.

**14. Notes shape.** "NC: <verdict>. <cites with quotes>. <battery SUMMARY line(s), copied>. <pointers>. Basis: section-open. Each cited section read whole 2026-11-07 from the Claude Code corpus corpus-NC-20261106 (official ncleg.gov text, General Statutes with changes through S.L. 2026-30, session laws 2024-2026, NCAC chapters as served by OAH; hashes verified and six sections matched to the live site, gaps log §1)." Verdicts (rule 36): "Present", "Present in part", "Confirmed absent", "Not located". A Not-located row names the missing chapter and section in its body.

**15. Absence rows (rule 19).** The body says what North Carolina law does not have, then the general law that does apply (with pointers), then what remains local. The battery: control run, at least one known positive per pattern (a real section the pattern must hit, or a synthetic sentence in the statute's own verbs), both word orders, windows of at least 1,200 characters plus a section-wide AND where a window could miss, `[\s\S]` for spans, `\b` on short words, the inflection and heading-only warnings read, session laws in scope, and for duties that would reach any person at least one pattern with no tenancy word. Trap words: lease/release/please, late/related/translate, cure/secure/procure, rent/current/parent, tenant/lieutenant, let/letter/outlet, deposit/depository, pet/petition/competent. Rerun a changed battery under a new id with `--supersedes`.

#### Main-pass decisions (NC items 5-8)

**Item 5, `keys`.** No conflict. The clause lets Landlord re-key and "charge the cost to Tenant" only when Tenant fails to return keys or needs a replacement. That is a cost of the tenant's act, not an administrative fee: § 42-46 governs late fees, the three eviction administrative fees, eviction out-of-pocket costs and required insurance, and 'the term "administrative fees" does not include out-of-pocket expenses, litigation costs, or other fees' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-46(k)), so § 42-46(h)(4) does not reach it; § 42-42(a)(8) lets the landlord 'recover from the tenant the actual and reasonable costs of repairs that are the fault of the tenant' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42(a)(8)) after fixing missing locks (f., g.). A protected tenant's lock change: 'The protected tenant shall bear the expense of changing the locks.' and must give the landlord a key 'within 48 hours' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.3(c)); the clause's consent rule covers duplicating keys, not the statutory lock change, and `no-alterations` preserves it. No wording change proposed.

**Item 5, `hoa-compliance`.** No conflict. Association fines are imposed on the lot or unit owner after notice and a hearing ('The lot owner charged shall be given notice of the charge, opportunity to be heard and to present evidence, and notice of the decision.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 47F-3-107.1); the same for condominiums, N.C. Gen. Stat. § 47C-3-107.1), capped at $100 a day, and 'Such fines shall be assessments secured by liens' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 47F-3-107.1). Nothing in Chapter 42 bars a lease making the tenant responsible for a fine caused by the tenant's own violation; it is not a § 42-46 fee (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-46(k)), and an unpaid fine that becomes a lien is a permitted deposit use ('Any unpaid bills that become a lien against the demised property due to the tenant's occupancy.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-51(a)(4))). No wording change proposed.

**Item 6.**
- `smoking-policy`: reads correctly. No North Carolina statute limits a private lease smoking ban (screen: every Chapter 42 and 42A hit for "smok" is a smoke-alarm duty, §§ 42-42(a)(5), 42-43, 42-44(a1), 42-51(a)(2), 42A-31 to 42A-33); remediation cost for smoking by Tenant, an occupant or a guest is a cost, not a § 42-46 fee.
- `no-alterations`: reads correctly. The final sentence preserves the fair-housing reasonable-modification right (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 41A-4(f)(1)) and the protected tenant's lock change (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.3(c)); no North Carolina statute found that makes a tenant installation the tenant's property.
- `addendum-precedence`: reads correctly. No North Carolina statute prescribes a residential lease addendum or notice that controls over the lease (screen: every "addendum" hit in the General Statutes and 2024-2026 session laws is outside residential leasing; the closest, N.C. Gen. Stat. § 47E-5, is a sale disclosure). The option-to-purchase protections apply 'Notwithstanding any option contract or covered lease agreement provisions to the contrary' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 47G-4), and the clause's "applicable law says controls" sentence carries that.

**Item 7(a), `early-termination-ks`.** North Carolina needs its own flat-fee override, as GA did; proposed in §6 as `early-termination-nc` (one month's Rent, deposit applied only to actual damages, no fee on a statutory termination under §§ 42-45 (military technicians), 42-45.1 or 42-45.2, or on a servicemember's termination under N.C. Gen. Stat. § 127B-28 and the federal Act).

**Item 7(b).** `holdover-ca` and `holdover-rate-nc` read correctly: a tenant who 'holds over after his term has expired' may be removed by summary ejectment 'after demand made for its surrender' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-26(a)(1)), and the landlord 'may claim rent in arrears, and damages for the occupation of the premises since the cessation of the estate of the lessee' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-28); no statute sets or bars an agreed holdover charge (`edu-no-holdover-multiplier-nc`). Currency: neither section amended since 2012/1995; S.L. 2025-88 cites § 42-26 only to exclude holdover tenants from its new unauthorized-occupant removal procedure (effective December 1, 2025).

**Item 7(c).** No NC eviction row contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`. North Carolina's own periods (the 10-day demand in N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-3; seven days for month to month in N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-14) are stated in `edu-eviction-process-nc`, `edu-periodic-tenancy-notice-nc`, `default-by-tenant-nc` and `periodic-tenancy-notice-nc` as state rules; none says no other notice applies, and the federal row is written as an addition to the state's notices.

**Item 8, arbitration: tag NC on `arbitration-agreement` (done in the delta).** North Carolina meets Taylor's offer rule without relying on federal preemption: 'An agreement contained in a record to submit to arbitration any existing or subsequent controversy arising between the parties to the agreement is valid, enforceable, and irrevocable except upon a ground that exists at law or in equity for revoking a contract.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 1-569.6(a)), with no consumer or residential exclusion (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 1-569.3); § 22B-10 bars jury waivers but 'does not prohibit parties from entering into agreements to arbitrate' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 22B-10); § 22B-3 voids only an out-of-state forum, and the clause sits in the Premises' county. No statute penalizes including a void term: §§ 42-25.8 and 42-46(h)(4) make it void and nothing more, and no Chapter 42 section makes it an unfair trade practice (Batteries NC-M-01, NC-M-02, §3). Chapter 75 liability for a lease term would be case law, not read. Answers `binding-arbitration` with the tag and `edu-binding-arbitration-nc`.

**§ 42-46(i)(4) garble (raised again by the canvass).** Known (NC log §1.1; kickoff item 11). The live ncleg.gov § 42-46 page matched the corpus text by whitespace-free SHA-256 on 2026-11-07 (gaps log §1.1), so the live site prints the same fragment; every row relying on § 42-46(i) follows the enacted text of S.L. 2025-52, s. 3(a) and S.L. 2025-54, s. 12.4(a). Not repeated as an SOP proposal.

**New `{{variables}}` (rule 60).** None. New rows reuse `{{property_address}}`, `{{county}}`, `{{tenant_maintained_items}}`, `{{maintenance_agreement_consideration}}` and `{{monthly_rent}}`, all already in the library.

**Open question to Taylor (rule 12, asked 2026-11-07).** May the pass save the standard federal sections (uscode.house.gov, eCFR) through the built-in browser? Recommendation: yes. No answer yet, so rows point to existing federal library rows and cite no unread U.S.C. or C.F.R. section; the federal points with no library row are listed in §7.

**Review status.** One row stays NEEDS_REVIEW: `edu-pest-control-notice-nc`, because 02 NCAC 34 and 02 NCAC 09L are not in the corpus and the official text could not be reached (§1.1). `edu-fire-code-standard-nc` was moved to VERIFIED after the State Fire Marshal's letter was read (§1.1).

#### Questions raised by the canvass
- `eminent-domain`: Should NC stay tagged on the shared optional clause `eminent-domain-vi`, whose award limb gives the leasehold's share of a condemnation award to Landlord? The tenant is an 'Owner' entitled to compensation (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 40A-2(5), (7)); no North Carolina statute bars or validates the assignment, so its enforceability is case law not read. Recommendation: Keep the tag (done in the delta), with the doubt stated in its NC segment. Class decision 2's 'the library offers no waiver clause' is aimed at waivers of the tenant-protection statutes; allocating a condemnation award is an ordinary contract term that 12 other states' tags carry, it is optional, and no North Carolina statute penalizes including a void term (Battery NC-M-01). If you prefer the stricter reading, remove NC from `eminent-domain-vi` at sync; `edu-eminent-domain-nc` answers the topic on its own.

### 5. Independent check
Method (rule 80, scaled down per the kickoff: at most two rounds, one agent at a time). Round 1 ran as five sequential parts, each by a fresh agent that had not seen the work, given only `CHECK-BRIEF` instructions, the class decisions and the answer objects: every row and tag, read sentence by sentence against the whole printed section, with currency, battery adequacy (rule 19, the checker's own unlogged screens with different words), fields and basis. Errata were exact old→new strings with the statute words; each was confirmed to occur once before it was applied by script, then the checker for each later part was told the error shapes found so far.

| Part | Objects | OK | With errata | Errata |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A (main pass, S01, S02) | 50 | 31 | 19 | 24 |
| B (S03, S04) | 51 | 28 | 23 | 29 |
| C (S05, S06) | 54 | 23 | 31 | 59 |
| D (S07, S08) | 60 | 31 | 29 | 43 |
| E (S09, S10) | 64 | 29 | 35 | 45 |
| Total | 279 | 142 | 137 | 200 |

All 200 round-1 errata were applied (0 failed to match). Recurring shapes: statutory conditions, qualifiers and scope limits dropped from bodies (rule 79; e.g. §§ 42-3, 42-12, 42-42(a)(8), 42-46(h)(5), 160D-1203, 160D-1207(c)); absence claims beyond what was searched (State Building Code, local ordinances and NCAC chapters outside the corpus are now named as not read); doubts stated as settled; § 42-45 overstated as covering servicemembers (it reaches 'Any military technician under section 10216 of Title 10 of the United States Code'; servicemembers come under N.C. Gen. Stat. § 127B-28 and the federal Act); lists of allowed eviction charges missing the § 42-46(i)(4) frivolous-appeal fees; § 47E-2(b)(2) read backwards; any-person rules missed by batteries tied to tenancy words (fixed in the bodies with the sections found, e.g. N.C. Gen. Stat. §§ 14-361.1, 95-223 to 95-226, 143-443(b), 47G-2(a), 14-318.5(c)). One method defect: the guest-rights battery NC-S02-32 ran a pattern in one word order; rerun as NC-M-03 (both orders), conclusion unchanged.

Main-pass edits after round 1, besides the errata: `edu-guest-rights-nc` notes (NC-M-03); `lease-copy-receipt-hi` segment (a clause phrase moved from single to double quotes); the proposed `early-termination-nc` (§6, not in the delta) narrowed to military technicians.

Round 2: the 137 objects edited after round 1, each given to a re-checker with its previous version to diff against (rule 80), in two sequential parts. Part a (70 objects): 62 OK, 8 with errata (8 errata), mostly in the round-1 replacement text itself (a duty turned into an option, an exception stated flatly, a scope widened, a second limb dropped), all applied. Part b (78 objects: the other 67, plus the eight part-a errata objects and three objects whose batteries were rerun after part a, re-queued with their newer versions): 61 OK, 17 with errata (19 errata), all applied. Part b also judged the five reruns: NC-M-04 and NC-M-05 met rule 19; NC-M-07, NC-M-09 and NC-M-10 did not (windows below 1,200 characters, no section-wide AND, a bare "season"), and were rerun as NC-M-12 (lease copy), NC-M-13 (police cooperation) and NC-M-11, then NC-M-14 (seasonal; NC-M-11's real positive § 95-223 failed because the Migrant Housing Act uses no tenancy word, so housing limbs without one were added). Every hit of the new runs was read; no verdict changed.

Before delivery every logged battery term was re-run against the corpus: 1,292 terms, all reproduced (control "zqxvbnmwt" 0 in every run). The quote checker passed on all 279 objects (2,296 quotes) and the assembler found every topic answered, every pointer resolved and no working-file reference.

**Rows edited after the last round (17; not re-checked, as the kickoff caps the check at two rounds):** `edu-confirmed-absences-misc-nc`, `edu-foreign-ownership-nc`, `edu-law-enforcement-cooperation-nc`, `edu-lease-copy-nc`, `edu-no-rent-receipt-anti-waiver-nc`, `edu-nonrefundable-deposit-notice-nc`, `edu-notice-service-fee-nc`, `edu-nuisance-nc`, `edu-part5-nonwaivable-nc`, `edu-prohibited-lease-terms-nc`, `edu-repair-cost-termination-nc`, `edu-required-fees-nc`, `edu-seasonal-tenancy-nc`, `edu-senior-housing-work-card-nc`, `edu-truth-in-renting-nc`, `extended-absence-notice-ks`, `lease-copy-receipt-hi`. Each carries round-2 errata applied exactly as the checker wrote them; `edu-seasonal-tenancy-nc`, `edu-lease-copy-nc`, `edu-law-enforcement-cooperation-nc`, `edu-confirmed-absences-misc-nc`, `lease-copy-receipt-hi` also carry the rule 19 reruns NC-M-12, NC-M-13 and NC-M-14 in their notes.

### 6. Findings on existing rows
- `early-termination-ks`. Row says: "Tenant will pay an early termination fee equal to one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}) or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater". Statute: 'nor may the landlord retain an amount from the security deposit which exceeds his actual damages' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-52); 'The tenant is not liable for any other rent or fees due only to the early termination of the tenancy.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-45.1(b)). Item 7(a): North Carolina needs its own flat-fee override, as GA did. The shared clause is not contrary to a North Carolina statute (its last sentence preserves §§ 42-45, 42-45.1 and 42-45.2), but its fee scales with the remaining term (a liquidated-damages penalty risk that only case law could settle), and it does not say that the deposit may cover the fee only up to actual damages (§ 42-52) or that the statutory terminations carry no fee. Recommend the override below (`early-termination-nc`, supersedes `early-termination-ks` for NC), with NC removed from `early-termination-ks` at sync.
  Proposed row (all fields):
  ```json
  {"id": "early-termination-nc", "group": "Default & Termination", "title": "Early Termination", "states": "NC", "is_active": "TRUE", "supersedes": "early-termination-ks", "bodyText": "Tenant may terminate this Lease before the end of the Term by providing Landlord at least 30 days' written notice. Tenant will pay Landlord, by the termination date, an early termination fee equal to one month's Rent ({{monthly_rent}}), and remains responsible for Rent and other obligations up to the termination date. If Tenant gives that notice, pays the fee and delivers possession of the property by the termination date, Tenant owes no Rent for any period after the termination date. Landlord may apply the Security Deposit to an unpaid early termination fee only to the extent of Landlord's actual damages from the early termination. If Tenant leaves before the end of the Term without using this option, Tenant's liability is governed by North Carolina law. This early-termination option and fee apply only if this Lease has a fixed Term; a month-to-month or other periodic tenancy ends on the notice that applicable law and this Lease provide, without an early termination fee. The fee does not apply when Tenant ends this Lease under a termination right North Carolina law gives, including a termination by a protected tenant who is a victim of domestic violence, sexual assault or stalking, a termination by a military technician (where only the limited amount the statute allows may be charged), or a termination after notice of a foreclosure sale of the property; in those cases Tenant owes Rent only as that law provides. Landlord may terminate this Lease early in accordance with this Lease's Tenant Default and notice provisions, or if Tenant vacates or abandons the property without notifying Landlord. Nothing in this Section limits any right either party has under applicable law, including a Tenant's right to terminate without penalty due to active military service under the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act or due to the property becoming uninhabitable through no fault of Tenant.", "rule_type": "REQUIRED", "content_type": "LEASE_CLAUSE", "verification_status": "VERIFIED", "notes": "NC: Override of `early-termination-ks` (NC gaps pass 2026-11-07, North Carolina item 7(a)), following `early-termination-ga`. Flat one-month fee instead of the greater of one month or 30% of the remaining Rent: no North Carolina statute regulates a fixed-term early-termination fee, its enforceability as liquidated damages is case law not read, and a fee that scales with the remaining term is the cautious-course risk; the legislature's own measure for a military termination is 'no greater than one month's rent' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-45(b)). Deposit: damages for 'the nonfulfillment of the rental period' are a permitted use 'except where the tenant terminated the rental agreement under G.S. 42-45, G.S. 42-45.1' or was forced out or constructively evicted (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-51(a)(3)), and the landlord may not 'retain an amount from the security deposit which exceeds his actual damages' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-52). Statutory terminations: domestic violence, sexual assault or stalking, 'The tenant is not liable for any other rent or fees due only to the early termination of the tenancy.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-45.1(b)), not waivable (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-45.1(d)); military technicians only ('Any military technician under section 10216 of Title 10 of the United States Code' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-45(c)); foreclosure of property with fewer than 15 rental units, 'The tenant is not liable for any other rent or damages due only to the early termination of the tenancy.' (N.C. Gen. Servicemembers generally: 'The rights, benefits, and protections of the federal Servicemembers Civil Relief Act ... apply to a servicemember engaged in military service' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 127B-28(a)); FEDERAL: the Servicemembers Civil Relief Act, see `edu-servicemember-rights-nc`. Proposed in gaps log §6 for Taylor's approval at sync; not in the delta. Basis: section-open. Each cited section read whole 2026-11-07 from the Claude Code corpus corpus-NC-20261106 (official ncleg.gov text, General Statutes with changes through S.L. 2026-30, session laws 2024-2026, NCAC chapters as served by OAH; hashes verified and six sections matched to the live site, gaps log §1).", "effective_from": "2026-11-07", "last_checked": "2026-11-07", "choice_group": "", "is_default": "", "topic_key": "early-termination", "lease_clause_basis": "SERVES_LANDLORD"}
  ```

- `edu-landlord-repair-duties-nc`. Row says: "These duties cannot be waived: a tenant's acceptance of a defective unit does not release the landlord, except that the parties may make a separate written contract, supported by consideration other than the rent, for the tenant to do specified work.". Statute: 'The landlord is not released of his obligations under any part of this section by the tenant's explicit or implicit acceptance of the landlord's failure to provide premises complying with this section, whether done before the lease was made, when it was made, or after it was made, unless a governmental subdivision imposes an impediment to repair for a specific period of time not to exceed six months' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42(b)); 'the landlord and tenant are not prohibited from making a subsequent written contract wherein the tenant agrees to perform specified work on the premises, provided that said contract is supported by adequate consideration other than the letting of the premises and is not made with the purpose or effect of evading the landlord's obligations under this Article' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42(b)). 
  Proposed wording: These duties cannot be waived: a tenant's acceptance of a defective unit, before, at or after the signing of the lease, does not release the landlord (except while a government body blocks a repair, for no more than six months). After the lease is made, the parties may make a later written contract for the tenant to do specified work, but only if it is supported by adequate consideration other than the letting of the home and is not made with the purpose or effect of evading the landlord's duties.
- `eviction-fees-nc`. Row says: "S.L. 2025-52, s. 3 and S.L. 2025-54, s. 12.4 (both approved 2025-07-02, each 'effective retroactively to September 9, 2024')". Statute: 'This section, being a clarifying amendment, has a retroactive effective date of September 9, 2024' (S.L. 2025-52, s. 3(b)); 'This section is effective retroactively to September 9, 2024' (S.L. 2025-54, s. 12.4(b)). 
  Proposed wording: S.L. 2025-52, s. 3 and S.L. 2025-54, s. 12.4 (both approved 2025-07-02; S.L. 2025-52, s. 3(b): 'This section, being a clarifying amendment, has a retroactive effective date of September 9, 2024'; S.L. 2025-54, s. 12.4(b): 'This section is effective retroactively to September 9, 2024')
- `edu-dv-lease-termination-nc`. Row says: "or within 72 hours of receiving a stay-away order if they are (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.3)". Statute: 'A landlord who receives a request under this subsection shall change the locks to the protected tenant's dwelling unit or give the protected tenant permission to change the locks within 72 hours' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.3(b)); 'Before the landlord or tenant changes the locks under this subsection, the tenant must provide the landlord with a copy of an order issued by a court that orders the perpetrator to stay away from the dwelling unit' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.3(b)(1)). 
  Proposed wording: or within 72 hours of the request if they are, and only after the tenant gives you a copy of a court order telling the perpetrator to stay away from the unit (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.3)
- `edu-dv-lease-termination-nc`. Row says: "You may not terminate, refuse to renew or retaliate because of victim status or use of these rights, which can't be waived.". Statute: 'A landlord shall not terminate a tenancy, fail to renew a tenancy, refuse to enter into a rental agreement, or otherwise retaliate in the rental of a dwelling based substantially on: (i) the tenant, applicant, or a household member's status as a victim of domestic violence, sexual assault, or stalking; or (ii) the tenant or applicant having terminated a rental agreement under G.S. 42-45.1' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.2); 'The provisions of this section may not be waived or modified by agreement of the parties' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-45.1(d)); N.C. Gen. Stat. §§ 42-42.2 and 42-42.3 carry no non-waiver sentence and Article 5 has none generally (gaps log §4 class decision 2).. 
  Proposed wording: You may not terminate, refuse to renew, refuse to rent or otherwise retaliate based substantially on victim status or on a tenant's having ended a lease under this termination right (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-42.2). The termination right can't be waived or modified by agreement (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-45.1(d)); the lock-change and nondiscrimination sections have no anti-waiver sentence of their own, and whether a lease waiver of them is enforceable is case law this library has not reviewed.
- `edu-security-deposit-rules-nc`. Row says: "and the tenant must be told in writing, within 30 days after the lease term begins, where the money is (or who issued the bond).". Statute: 'The landlord or the landlord's agent shall notify the tenant within 30 days after the beginning of the lease term of the name and address of the bank or institution where the tenant's deposit is currently located or the name of the insurance company providing the bond.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-50). 
  Proposed wording: and the tenant must be notified, within 30 days after the lease term begins, of the name and address of the bank or institution where the deposit is held (or the name of the insurance company providing the bond); the statute does not say how, so give the notice in writing. [Reason: N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-50 (read whole 2026-11-07) requires notice of the name and address but says nothing about writing; the row states a writing requirement the statute does not impose. The clause `security-deposit-holding-nc` already commits to writing, which is fine as the landlord's own choice.]
- `edu-security-deposit-rules-nc`. Row says: "If the tenant's address is unknown, the landlord may apply the deposit after 30 days and must hold the balance for at least six months.". Statute: 'If the tenant's address is unknown the landlord shall apply the deposit as permitted in G.S. 42-51 after a period of 30 days and the landlord shall hold the balance of the deposit for collection by the tenant for at least six months.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-52). 
  Proposed wording: If the tenant's address is unknown, the landlord must, after 30 days, apply the deposit to the permitted charges and hold the balance for the tenant to collect for at least six months. [Reason: the statute says "shall apply", not "may"; the S03 rows `edu-deposit-escheat-nc` and `edu-no-deposit-surrender-notice-nc` use the corrected wording.]
- `due-at-signing`. Row says: "Pet Deposit ({{pet_deposit}}) due at signing; last month's Monthly Rent due on the Start Date". Statute: 'The security deposit shall not exceed an amount equal to two weeks' rent if a tenancy is week to week' ... 'two months' rent for terms greater than month to month' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-51(b)); 'the landlord may charge a reasonable, nonrefundable fee for pets kept by the tenant on the premises' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-53).
