- `edu-landlord-repair-duties-nc`. Row says: "Large cities (200,000 or more people) must also require a heat source able to keep one room at 68 degrees.". Statute: 'This section applies only to local governments with a population of 200,000 or over within their planning and development regulation jurisdiction, according to the most recent decennial federal census' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 160D-1204(d)); 'so as to heat at least one habitable room, excluding the kitchen, to a minimum temperature of 68 degrees Fahrenheit measured 3 feet above the floor with an outside temperature of 20 degrees Fahrenheit' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 160D-1204(a)); 'Local government. - A city or county.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 160D-102(22)) (S.L. 2026-59, s. 49(a) adds subdivisions (16a) to (16c) to that section, uncompiled, with no renumbering). 
  Proposed wording: Cities and counties with 200,000 or more people in their planning and development jurisdiction must also require by ordinance a heat source able to heat at least one habitable room other than the kitchen to 68 degrees (measured 3 feet above the floor when it is 20 degrees outside), which the owner must keep in good and safe working order. (The section's subject is "local governments", not only cities, and the room excludes the kitchen; see `edu-heating-nc`.)
- `edu-meth-decontamination-nc`. Row says: "Decontamination rules (15A NCAC) not read (instruction 16).". Statute: 'The rules of this Subchapter implement the provisions of G.S.130A-284 by establishing decontamination standards for property that has been used for the manufacture of methamphetamine.' (10A NCAC 41D .0101). 
  Proposed wording: Replace with: "Decontamination rules are 10A NCAC 41D .0101 to .0105 (read 2026-11-07 in the corpus): 'The contaminated property shall not be occupied prior to decontamination of the property in accordance with these Rules.' (10A NCAC 41D .0101); a responsible party (owner, lessee, operator or other person in control with knowledge) must, before habitation, do a pre-decontamination assessment, decontaminate and document (10A NCAC 41D .0101(b)), notify the local health department on completion and provide the documentation (10A NCAC 41D .0104); where law enforcement has posted the property, the local health department must inform the owner of record that the property 'must be vacated' (10A NCAC 41D .0101(d)). None of these rules requires disclosure to a tenant." The body is correct as written; only the notes' citation (15A NCAC, not read) is wrong.
- `utility-billing-nc`. Row says: "hot-water allocation only for pre-1989 contiguous units, with bill contents and a 25-day past-due date". Statute: 'If the leased premises are contiguous dwelling units built prior to 1989, {+or a mobile home located within a mobile home park sited prior to 1989, +}and the lessor determines that the measurement of the lessee's total water usage is impractical or not economical' (S.L. 2026-59, s. 52), which also strikes '[-A lessor shall not utilize a ratio utility billing system or other allocation billing system that does not rely on individually submetered hot water usage to determine the allocation of water and sewer costs.-]' (S.L. 2026-59, s. 52); 'Except as otherwise provided, this act is effective when it becomes law.' (S.L. 2026-59, s. 53.(b)); approved August 11, 2026; uncompiled in N.C. Gen. Stat. § 62-110(g). The Commission's rules still print the ban: 'The provider shall not utilize a ratio utility billing system or other allocation billing system that does not rely on individually submetered hot water usage to determine the allocation of water and sewer usage.' (04 NCAC 11 R18-08).. 
  Proposed wording: Notes only (body unaffected). Replace the row words with: "hot-water allocation only for pre-1989 contiguous units and, since August 11, 2026, a mobile home in a mobile home park sited before 1989 (S.L. 2026-59, s. 52, uncompiled; effective when it became law under S.L. 2026-59, s. 53.(b)), with bill contents and a 25-day past-due date; the same act struck sub-subdivision (1a)a., the statutory ratio-billing bar, but added no authority for ratio billing, and 04 NCAC 11 R18-06 and R18-08 still forbid it (see `edu-utility-apportionment-nc`)". Also replace "S.L. 2026-59, s. 52 amended N.C. Gen. Stat. § 62-110(g) for pre-1989 mobile home parks (deprioritized layer)." with "S.L. 2026-59, s. 52 rewrote N.C. Gen. Stat. § 62-110(g)(1a) as stated above." The clause body (billing method "as authorized by the North Carolina Utilities Commission") stays correct.
- `utility-billing-nc`. Row says: "and will not end this Lease because of unpaid water, sewer or electric charges.". Statute: 'No charge for connection or disconnection or late fee or deposit paid by the provider to the supplier shall be allowed, and no provider may terminate a lease for nonpayment of natural gas service.' (04 NCAC 11 R24-07); the clause's own bracket covers natural gas billing ("[Use only if Landlord bills Tenant for water or sewer, electric, or natural gas service, or for a central system.]", library text), and its notes cite N.C. Gen. Stat. § 62-110(i). Electricity: 'no provider may terminate a lease for nonpayment of electric service' (04 NCAC 11 R22-07).. 
  Proposed wording: Replace with: "and will not end this Lease because of unpaid water, sewer, electric or natural gas charges." As written, a clause used for natural gas billing promises no-termination only for water, sewer and electricity, implying a right the Commission's natural gas rule denies. Add to the NC notes: 'no provider may terminate a lease for nonpayment of natural gas service' (04 NCAC 11 R24-07).
- `edu-utility-billing-nc`. Row says: "may not end the lease because of unpaid water, sewer or electric charges". Statute: 'no provider may terminate a lease for nonpayment of natural gas service' (04 NCAC 11 R24-07), alongside 'An arrearage in costs owed by a tenant for water or sewer services pursuant to G.S. 62-110(g) or electric service pursuant to G.S. 62-110(h) shall not be used as a basis for termination of a lease under this Chapter.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-26(b)).. 
  Proposed wording: Replace with: "may not end the lease because of unpaid water, sewer, electric or natural gas charges". The row's first sentence covers natural gas billing, so the omission reads as permission. Add to the NC notes the Commission rule 04 NCAC 11 R24-07 (read 2026-11-07) beside N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-26(b). No other sentence of this row is changed by S.L. 2026-59, s. 52: its water sentence ("generally metered use at no more than the supplier's rate") stays correct, since N.C. Gen. Stat. § 62-110(g)(1) is not amended.
- `edu-no-plain-language-law-nc`. Row says: "North Carolina has no plain-language or readability statute for residential leases, no type-size or bold-print rule for them, and no statute requiring a lease to list the rights a tenant gives up.". Statute: 'A conspicuous statement, in not less than 14-point boldface type, immediately above the purchaser's signature, that the purchaser has the right to cancel the contract at anytime until midnight of the third business day following execution of the option contract or delivery of the contract, whichever occurs last.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 47G-2(b)(9)); 'Covered lease agreement or lease agreement. - A residential lease agreement that is combined with, or is executed concurrently with, an option contract.' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 47G-1(1)); 'clearly and conspicuously on the notification in at least 12 point type and in bold print' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 75-41(a)(4)). 
  Proposed wording: North Carolina has no plain-language or readability statute for residential leases, no type-size or bold-print rule for the lease as a whole, and no statute requiring a lease to list the rights a tenant gives up. (An option to purchase combined with or signed with the lease has its own 14-point boldface cancellation statement, and an automatic-renewal notice, if that statute reaches a lease, has a 12-point bold rule; see edu-lease-type-size-nc.)
- `edu-vacation-rental-scope-nc`. Row says: "Rentals by hotels, motels, campgrounds and similar lodging to the same guest for fewer than 90 consecutive days are 'transient occupancies' outside Chapter 42 entirely and create no tenancy unless the agreement says so.". Statute: 'occupancy or emergency or disaster declared occupancy.' and '(d) For the purposes of this section, an "emergency or disaster declared occupancy" is the rental of an accommodation by an apartment, inn, hotel, motel, recreational vehicle park, campground, or similar lodging to the same guest or occupant (i) as a result of an emergency declared by the Governor or General Assembly pursuant to G.S. 166A-19.20, a disaster declared by the Governor pursuant to G.S. 166A-19.21, or a major disaster declared by the President pursuant to the Stafford Act (P.L. 93-288), and (ii) who is a recipient of Community Development Block Grant-Disaster Recovery assistance and is renting the accommodation during the repair or reconstruction of the guest's or occupant's home through a program administered by the Department of Commerce.' (S.L. 2026-41, s. 5A.17, rewriting N.C. Gen. Stat. § 72-1, uncompiled; 'Except as otherwise provided, this act becomes effective July 1, 2026.' (S.L. 2026-41, s. 45.8)); 'The provisions of this Chapter shall not apply to transient occupancies, as defined in G.S. 72-1(c).' (N.C. Gen. Stat. § 42-14.6). 
  Proposed wording: Keep the sentence and add after it: "Since July 1, 2026, the innkeeper statute also covers an 'emergency or disaster declared occupancy', which can include an apartment's rental to a disaster-recovery grant recipient while the occupant's own home is repaired; the Chapter 42 exclusion still names only transient occupancies, so whether such an apartment stay is a tenancy is unsettled, and the cautious course is to treat it as one." Notes: cite S.L. 2026-41, s. 5A.17 and s. 45.8 (approved July 7, 2026) and say the compiled N.C. Gen. Stat. § 72-1 does not yet show the change.
- `edu-no-cash-receipt-duty-nc`. Row says: "North Carolina has no statute requiring a landlord to give a receipt for rent, including cash rent, or regulating how rent may be paid.". Statute: 'A broker shall create, maintain or retain, as required by Rule .0108 of this Section, the following records:' and '(8) copies of leases, security deposit checks, property management agreements, property management statements, and receipts for cash payments in leasing transactions' (21 NCAC 58A .0117(c)). 
  Proposed wording: North Carolina has no statute requiring a landlord to give a receipt for rent, including cash rent, or regulating how rent may be paid. A licensed real estate broker who manages rentals must keep copies of receipts for cash payments in leasing transactions among its trust-account records, so a broker-managed property should give a receipt for every cash payment. (Notes: cite 21 NCAC 58A .0117(c)(8) and Battery NC-S09-22.)

### 7. Findings for other states
- ALL `(federal)`: FEDERAL (all states; class decision 8): rows cite no U.S.C. or C.F.R. section the pass did not read, and these federal points have no shared library row to point to. Candidates for shared federal education rows, if Taylor approves saving federal sections: VAWA housing protections and confidentiality in federally assisted housing (`edu-dv-eviction-protection-nc`, `edu-dv-confidentiality-nc`); federal fair housing reasonable-modification and escrow rules (`edu-disability-accommodation-nc`); the federal housing-for-older-persons exemption (`edu-senior-housing-work-card-nc`); federal consumer-report and fair-housing screening rules (`edu-tenant-screening-nc`); federal furnisher duties for rent reporting (`edu-rent-reporting-nc`); the bankruptcy automatic stay and lease assumption (`edu-tenant-bankruptcy-nc`); the FCC over-the-air reception devices rule (`edu-no-telecom-access-rule-nc`); Housing Choice Voucher inspection, access and housing-assistance-payment abatement rules (`edu-no-subsidized-inspection-refusal-rule-nc`, `edu-no-subsidy-proration-rule-nc`); HUD and USDA fee rules for assisted housing (`edu-required-fees-nc`); and federal approval of leases of tribal trust land (`edu-communal-land-lease-approval-nc`).

### Proposed SOP changes
- Rule 80 (NC-gaps): on a large gaps pass, run the independent check in parts as soon as each pair of slices is saved (still one checker at a time), and pass each part's recurring error shapes to the next checker part and to helpers still writing. NC evidence: part A found 24 errata in 19 of 50 objects, mostly qualifiers dropped from bodies (rule 79), the § 42-45 scope (military technicians only) and 'only these charges' lists missing § 42-46(i)(4); later parts were told to look for those shapes.
- Rule 16 (NC-gaps): a pass's list of sections changed after the compilation's last act must parse the acts' text with line wrapping undone and catch every form ('G.S. X reads as rewritten', 'Subdivision (1) of G.S. X', 'Article N of Chapter M ... reads as rewritten', chapter-wide word substitutions). NC's first parser missed 151 sections, among them §§ 47C-3-121 and 47F-3-121 (S.L. 2026-59, s. 4) and Article 27A of Chapter 14 (S.L. 2026-41, s. 20.2.(n)), because 'reads as rewritten' was split across lines. (Merges two helper proposals: S.L. 2026-41, s. 20.2.(n) rewrote all of Article 27A of Chapter 14, replacing "Department of Public Safety" with "Bureau" throughout the sex offender registration article, effective when it became law for reports, applications and fees on or after that date (s. 20.2.(z)); and S.L. 2026-59, s. 4 rewrote §§ 47C-3-121(1) and 47F-3-121(1), striking the 'unless' exceptions on flag display, from August 11, 2026; both were missed by the first list and are applied where rows rely on them.)
- Rule 27 (NC-gaps): the helper brief says from the start that a topic may carry both a tag of a fitting shared optional clause and an NC education row; one NC helper read 'one answer object per topic' as excluding a tag, and the main pass had to tag `rental-inspection-cooperation-mn` afterwards.

### Proposed topic questions
- `required-disclosures`: Should the library carry a North Carolina row (and possibly a clause) for option contracts combined with residential leases under Chapter 47G (N.C. Gen. Stat. §§ 47G-1 to 47G-7: signed and acknowledged contract with minimum terms, 14-point boldface three-business-day cancellation statement above the purchaser's signature, recording within five business days, once-a-year cure right, 30-day notice of default and intent to forfeit)? No library row cites Chapter 47G; this pass put the requirement in `edu-required-disclosures-nc`, but a lease-option topic would make it visible in the cross-state check.

# Topic gaps sync (Claude Code, 2026-11-07)

Merged on base 8fa1f6f: 250 new rows, 29 tags (279 records, matching the log). Spot-check on ncleg.gov (live BySection pages, 19 sections): every §6 quote matched except two paraphrases in the log's own wording (§ 42-51(b) 'if a tenancy is month to month'; § 42-45.2 'less than 15'), neither quoted in a row; the NCAC and session-law quotes matched in the corpus. The 17 rows edited after round 2 were re-checked by a Claude Code checker against the corpus: 9 hold; 12 fixes applied to 8 rows (`edu-foreign-ownership-nc` 2, `edu-law-enforcement-cooperation-nc` 2, `edu-nonrefundable-deposit-notice-nc`, `edu-nuisance-nc`, `edu-prohibited-lease-terms-nc` 2, `edu-repair-cost-termination-nc` 2, `edu-seasonal-tenancy-nc`, `edu-senior-housing-work-card-nc`), each quote confirmed in the corpus. §6: all 16 findings applied: `early-termination-nc` (NC untagged from `early-termination-ks`, as approved for GA and SC), `due-at-signing-nc` (NC untagged from `due-at-signing`), `edu-animal-fighting-lease-void-nc`, and wording fixes to `edu-landlord-repair-duties-nc` (2), `eviction-fees-nc`, `edu-dv-lease-termination-nc` (2), `edu-security-deposit-rules-nc` (2), `edu-meth-decontamination-nc`, `utility-billing-nc`, `edu-utility-billing-nc`, `edu-no-plain-language-law-nc`, `edu-vacation-rental-scope-nc`, `edu-no-cash-receipt-duty-nc`. Arbitration: NC tagged on `arbitration-agreement` (offer rule met; N.C. Gen. Stat. § 1-569.6(a); no penalty for a void term). `eminent-domain-vi` tag kept as recommended. Open: the federal-sections question (rule 12) and 44 rule-79 segments (backlog). SOP 2.00. One topic question added.

## Rule type: early termination (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision 2026-11-12: `early-termination-nc` is now `rule_type` RECOMMENDED, not REQUIRED, with the shared `early-termination-ks` and every state version that copied its label (OH, NV, TX, NJ, AZ, GA, SC, NC, VA, PA, IL). Under SOP rule 56, REQUIRED means a statute requires the clause in the lease, and no state's does. The label is research metadata only; no clause text changed. Taylor's lesson 2 (flag a lease without an early-termination clause) stays as a builder warning in the backlog.
